14:I[8669,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DateSidebar"]
15:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
16:T446,The paper focuses on UDDMs, where the forward process corrupts discrete tokens toward a uniform distribution and the reverse model learns conditional token distributions. The key conceptual move is to connect conditional likelihood maximization to associative memory. Classical Hopfield-style memories use explicit energy functions to guarantee stable attractors, but generic feed-forward networks and Transformers do not naturally satisfy the symmetry constraints required by such energy formulations. The authors instead use a pseudo-likelihood or conditional-likelihood perspective: if a model learns to reconstruct tokens from corrupted contexts, the induced reverse dynamics can form basins of attraction around data points. In a simplified setting, the analysis shows that maximizing conditional likelihood implicitly enforces a Hebbian-like update on data points and increases classification margins. Empirically, token recovery rate probes whether a corrupted sequence returns to a given sample, while conditional entropy measures how stable and confident the local token prediction is.17:T476,The experiments examine how the retrieval behavior changes as the training set size grows. In the memorization regime, the model reliably recovers training examples but treats unseen test sequences as unstable, often altering their tokens during reverse denoising. As more data are added, the basins around individual training examples become less dominant, and unseen in-distribution examples begin to acquire partial basins. In the generalized regime, the model tends to preserve uncorrupted test tokens and can recover tokens from partially corrupted test sequences. Token-level conditional entropy separates these behaviors: successfully recovered tokens have near-zero entropy, while unstable or incorrectly recovered tokens have higher entropy. Sequence-level conditional entropy is used as a practical transition detector; once the generalized regime is reached, the entropy distributions of training and synthetic sequences align. The provided text does not include concrete parameter counts, dataset sizes, or full numeric tables, so the safest reading is as a mechanism and diagnostic study rather than a scale-calibrated benchmark.18:T42e,They define a single-axis TSP group (size N_TSP) where each rank simultaneously owns a shard of the model weights and a shard of the token sequence.

Two main runtime schedules are described:
1) Attention: an iterative TSP loop that (a) broadcasts one rank’s packed attention projection weight shards (WQ/WK/WV/WO) to peers, (b) computes local projections on each rank’s sequence shard, and (c) performs sequence-wise K/V exchange (all-gather-like) to reconstruct the needed context for correct causal attention. A zigzag sequence partition is used to balance causal-attention workload across ranks.
2) Gated MLP: a ring-style point-to-point schedule motivated by the structural analogy between σ(X·W1^T)·W2 and softmax(Q·K^T)·V. Weight shards circulate via send/recv while each rank accumulates partial outputs locally, aiming to avoid the all-reduce commonly used in standard TP.

A topology argument is also central: since N_TSP ≤ N_TP·N_SP, the combined group is more likely to fit within intra-node bandwidth, whereas a 2D layout can spill across nodes.19:T890,Spin 的核心是把多种 sparse attention 方法抽象成统一的五阶段执行流水线：Index、Select、Attention、Offload、Retrieve。其中前三个是算法相关的计算步骤，后两个是共享的数据管理步骤。作者观察到，不同 sparse 方法虽然选择粒度不同，比如 block、cluster 或其他结构，但执行模式相似：先根据 query 生成索引或候选，再选出需要访问的 KV 子集，最后做注意力计算。因此系统层不必为每种算法单独写一套 runtime。

为实现这种统一，Spin 引入 partition 作为逻辑抽象层。partition 不是某一种具体稀疏单元，而是把算法定义的稀疏粒度映射到系统可管理的页式 KV 单元。这样做的好处是把“算法想访问什么”和“硬件适合怎样分配/传输”解耦。算法可以继续按自己的 block 或 cluster 语义工作，runtime 则按 page 粒度做分配、缓存和传输。

在 KV 管理上，Spin 重点解决 GPU-CPU 分层存储下的 locality 问题。作者认为 sparse decoding 不是完全随机访问，跨相邻 decoding step 往往存在时间局部性，因此可以通过 locality-aware replacement policy 提高 GPU residency。为此他们设计了 dynamic KV buffer，支持请求生命周期中的扩容、收缩和原地更新，同时维护适合 GPU 访问的元数据结构。

另一个关键点是 hierarchical metadata。长上下文下，哪怕 dense attention，页表和索引的元数据也会变大；到了 head-wise sparse retrieval，额外索引开销更明显。Spin 借鉴操作系统的多级索引思想，让元数据规模更接近物理 working set，而不是逻辑最大上下文空间，并把部分元数据放在 CPU 侧，减少 GPU 内存占用。

整体上，这篇工作的创新不在某个单一 kernel，而在于把 sparse attention、paged KV cache、GPU-CPU 分层存储、在线调度和元数据管理放进一个一致的系统设计里。和已有 vLLM 类 dense-serving 框架相比，它不再假设每步都要扫描全量 KV；和很多稀疏算法原型相比，它强调统一抽象和可复用 runtime，而不是为单一算法做特化实现。1a:T93c,Spin organizes sparse attention serving into a unified five-stage pipeline: Index, Select, Attention, Offload, and Retrieve. The first three are algorithm-specific compute stages, while the last two are shared data-management stages. The key observation is that many sparse attention methods differ mostly in the granularity of their selected units—blocks, clusters, and so on—while sharing the same high-level execution pattern: identify candidate regions, select the relevant KV subset, and run attention on that subset. This makes a common runtime feasible.

To support that runtime, Spin introduces partition as the central abstraction. A partition is not tied to one specific sparse method; instead, it maps algorithm-defined sparse units onto system-manageable page-based KV units. This decouples what the algorithm wants to access from how the hardware prefers to allocate, cache, and transfer memory. Sparse methods can preserve their own semantics, while the runtime handles memory movement at page granularity.

The second major component is locality-aware hierarchical KV management. The authors argue that sparse decoding is not purely random: there is temporal locality across adjacent decoding steps, so useful KV states can remain resident on the GPU. Spin therefore uses a dynamic KV buffer that supports per-request resizing and in-place updates as requests arrive, advance, and finish, while maintaining GPU-friendly metadata for replacement and lookup.

The third component is hierarchical metadata. At long context lengths, even dense attention incurs nontrivial page-table overhead, and head-wise sparse retrieval adds more indexing cost. Spin borrows multi-level indexing ideas from operating systems so that metadata scales with the physical working set rather than the worst-case logical context space. It also splits metadata across GPU and CPU to reduce on-device overhead.

The novelty is not a single kernel optimization. It is the co-design of sparse attention execution, paged KV caching, GPU–CPU hierarchy, online scheduling, and metadata management within one consistent framework. Compared with dense-serving systems such as standard vLLM-style runtimes, Spin no longer assumes full-KV scans at every step. Compared with one-off sparse prototypes, it emphasizes reusable abstractions rather than per-algorithm specialization.1b:T6c4,From the provided text, Spin is implemented on top of vLLM and integrates three representative sparse attention methods. The paper also defines an ideal sparse-serving envelope to quantify how far current implementations are from the theoretical upper bound. Although the excerpt does not include the full result tables, the experimental structure is clear.

First, the paper evaluates end-to-end serving performance, likely including throughput, per-token decode latency, and behavior under long contexts and higher concurrency. The relevant baselines should include dense serving and native implementations of the integrated sparse methods. The main question is whether the unified framework preserves and amplifies the algorithmic benefits of sparsity.

Second, it studies the gains from hierarchical KV management and locality-aware caching. Metrics here likely include GPU residency, PCIe transfer volume, cache hit rate, and retrieval latency. This is important because simply offloading KV to CPU and fetching on demand is not enough when accesses are irregular.

Third, it evaluates abstraction and metadata overhead. Partition abstraction and hierarchical metadata are only useful if their bookkeeping cost remains controlled at long context lengths and across different sparse methods.

The introduction gives a concrete scale example: a single 128K-token request for Qwen3-8B needs about 19GB of KV cache, while a sparse method with 95% sparsity would theoretically process less than 1GB of KV data per decoding step. That example makes the systems problem very tangible. Exact throughput gains, latency reductions, and consistency across sparse methods need to be checked in the main tables and the envelope-gap figures.1c:T4a4,For a quick read, start with the problem-definition figure around the ideal sparse-serving envelope. That figure explains why sparse attention’s theoretical savings often fail to materialize. Then read the system overview carefully, especially the five-stage pipeline and the partition abstraction, since those define the paper’s main interface. After that, focus on the KV manager and metadata sections: locality-aware replacement, dynamic KV buffers, and multi-level indexing each target a different bottleneck.

If you work on systems, it is worth reading the implementation details together with the experiments, especially which parts of a sparse algorithm need customization when integrated into Spin and which parts are reused. If your focus is foundation-model pretraining, this is more of a skim paper; 20–30 minutes should be enough. Two deeper questions are worth keeping in mind. Does the partition abstraction constrain future finer-grained or more structured sparsity patterns? And if training also starts to use hierarchical KV or approximate attention, could this serving abstraction feed back into model design by favoring more locality-friendly attention behavior?1d:T66f,The vulnerability is specific and well-scoped. In some frameworks, runtime activation quantization computes a single scale from the global min/max over the entire batched activation tensor. That means the victim’s activation magnitudes influence the quantization grid applied to the attacker’s sample. This, in turn, perturbs the attacker’s logits or top-1 log probabilities in a measurable way. By crafting inputs, repeatedly co-locating with the victim, and measuring those perturbations, the attacker can recover properties of the victim’s data.

The paper does not propose a new quantization algorithm; instead, it cleanly decomposes the attack surface. Static quantization is safe here because scales are fixed offline. Per-token dynamic quantization is also safe because each token gets its own scale, so there is no cross-sample coupling. The dangerous regime is per-tensor dynamic quantization, especially when the tensor spans the whole batch. The authors also explain why this mode still exists in practice: FP8 online quantization paths in serving frameworks, default APIs in general-purpose runtimes, and certain framework-specific configurations.

An important secondary point is that the paper does not frame security as a trade-off against performance. It argues that per-token dynamic quantization is often preferable anyway: on modern GPUs, per-token reductions map more naturally to parallel hardware and avoid expensive global synchronization, and in LLMs they usually preserve accuracy better because token activation distributions are highly heterogeneous. So the safer default is often also the better systems choice.1e:T5b7,The headline results are strong and easy to interpret. In LLM settings, the attack achieves 99.6% to 100% token recovery accuracy. In classification settings, it enables exact image identification. The attacker model is intentionally weak: it only requires co-location in the same batch and access to top-1 log probabilities, not internal activations or gradients.

The paper also surveys the vulnerability surface in real frameworks. It identifies risky configurations in vLLM’s default FP8 online quantization, SGLang’s per-tensor FP8 mode, and the dynamic quantization APIs of ONNX Runtime and PyTorch. By contrast, per-token dynamic quantization eliminates the side channel entirely. The authors further discuss deployment complications that may reduce exploitability in production, such as nondeterminism from hardware/software heterogeneity and provider-side randomization or scheduling changes, along with the tension between such mitigations and users’ expectation of deterministic temperature-0 behavior.

When reading the full experiments, the most useful details are whether the attack needs tight synchronization, how many repeated queries it needs, how success varies with batch size and quantization precision, and how default versus optional framework settings differ. Those details determine whether the issue is merely possible or dangerous under common defaults. From the introduction and abstract, the paper is clearly arguing the latter.1f:T404,This is a skim paper, but the threat model and mechanism are worth understanding clearly. Start with the introduction and background to pin down the necessary conditions: multi-tenant batching, dynamic activation quantization, and shared quantization parameters across the batch. Then jump to the attack method and main result tables, especially the LLM token-recovery setup and success rates. After that, read the framework survey carefully; that is the most actionable part because it tells you which common configurations are actually exposed.

A common reading mistake would be to generalize the result to all dynamic quantization. The paper is careful: the issue is concentrated in per-tensor dynamic quantization across the batch. Static quantization and per-token dynamic quantization are outside the attack surface. If deployment security is your concern, finish with the mitigation discussion and assess the operational cost of changing the default quantization granularity.

Estimated reading time is 20 to 30 minutes.20:T6e6,The core method is pipelined sharding. Instead of coarse layer-level offloading, the model is partitioned at sub-layer granularity. These shards are then scheduled across GPU and CPU depending on runtime conditions: execution phase, context length of in-flight requests, CPU thread count, PCIe bandwidth, and available VRAM.

The scheduler is benchmark/profile-guided rather than fully online-optimized. It precomputes timing estimates for a set of predefined token tiers, and for each tier selects the best among three candidate execution plans. The plans differ mainly in how aggressively they use PCIe transfers and whether they overlap large tensor movement with compute. Two plans allow movement of large tensors such as weights or KV cache and try to hide transfer cost behind concurrent CPU/GPU work. The third plan minimizes large PCIe traffic and restricts transfers mostly to small activations, which is preferable when bandwidth is tight or transfer overlap is not worthwhile.

A few design choices matter. It is not limited to single-request decode; it explicitly supports mixed batches where requests may be in different phases. VRAM budget is treated as a first-class runtime constraint. The abstraction also extends to MoE models, which suggests the scheduler is not overfit to dense transformers.

For VLMs, the paper combines pipelined sharding with three engineering optimizations, grouped as VLMOpt: offloading vision tensors to CPU, enabling flash attention in the vision encoder, and avoiding overlapping VRAM allocation peaks between the vision and language parts. These are not algorithmically new, but they are important because they reduce the vision-side memory peak enough to make high-resolution VLM inference feasible on client hardware.21:T558,From the full text excerpt, the evaluation spans dense LLMs, MoE LLMs, and high-resolution VLMs, and compares TTFT and TPS across different VRAM budgets, CPU thread counts, and CPU-GPU interconnect conditions. The paper is explicit that the goal is lossless inference, so the comparison focus is hybrid scheduling and offloading strategies rather than lossy quantization baselines.

The main empirical claim is that pipelined sharding is more robust than any single fixed strategy because it switches plans based on token tier and execution phase. It enables large dense and MoE models to run under low VRAM budgets, and with VLMOpt it makes high-resolution VLM inference practical in llama.cpp. The provided excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, but the framing suggests the key metrics are feasibility plus the TTFT/TPS trade-off, not just peak throughput.

When reading the full experiments, the most informative checks are: (1) phase-wise curves under different VRAM budgets, especially whether prefill or decode dominates; (2) whether MoE behavior is limited by expert-weight movement; and (3) memory/time breakdowns for the vision encoder, multimodal bridge, and language decoder under high-resolution inputs. The most useful ablations should be those that remove profile-guided tier selection, force a fixed plan, or disable one component of VLMOpt.22:Tb84,方法上有四个关键部分。第一是视觉编码器 CogViT。作者没有直接沿用通用 CLIP/ViT，而是做了两阶段预训练。阶段一是蒸馏式 masked image modeling，用 student ViT 重建被 mask 区域在双教师特征空间中的表示：SigLIP2 提供偏语义的表征，DINOv3 提供偏纹理/局部结构的表征。35% masking ratio，224×224 分辨率，数据配比是 80% 高质量自然图像、10% instruction-following data、10% scientific imagery。这里的设计意图很明确：先把视觉 backbone 的通用感知能力打牢，避免过早被图文对齐目标牵着走。训练上使用 Muon optimizer，并引入 QK-Norm 来抑制注意力 logit 爆炸，说明作者在大规模视觉预训练稳定性上做了针对性处理。阶段二转向 contrastive image-text pretraining，用 SigLIP loss 做图文对齐。升级点包括 NaFlex 可变分辨率输入、64K global batch 的大规模对比训练、以及 8B 中英双语图文语料。这里体现的是先表示、后对齐的路线，而不是从头只做对比学习。

第二是 MMTP，Multimodal Multi-Token Prediction。标准 MTP 在文本里很自然，因为 prefix token 可以直接嵌入后送入 MTP head；但多模态场景下，图像 token 怎么传给 MTP head 并不显然。作者比较了三种方案：直接传视觉 embedding；在 MTP head 里完全屏蔽视觉 token，退化成 text-only MTP；保留视觉位置信息，但把视觉 token 内容替换成共享可学习的 <|image|> token。最终采用第三种。原因是它在优化和系统效率之间更平衡：直接传视觉 embedding 会增加 head 侧负担和不稳定性；完全屏蔽视觉 token 又损失多模态条件信息；共享 special token 则保留“这里有图像”的结构信号和位置信息，同时避免把高维视觉特征直接灌进 MTP head。这个点很值得预训练研究员关注，因为它触及多模态 LM 训练目标如何与基础设施兼容的问题。

第三是多模态训练与深度融合。论文强调 vision-language integration 不是只在 SFT 阶段做，而是贯穿 pre-training 和 supervised fine-tuning。虽然公开内容没有把所有数据配比和 curriculum 细节完全展开，但可以看出作者在追求“视觉参与推理主干”，而不是“视觉先编码成几个 token 再让语言模型自己想办法用”。

第四是 joint RL 与 agent 基础设施。作者在 30 多类任务上做联合强化学习，覆盖 perception、reasoning、agentic capabilities，并配套优化了大规模 multimodal RL 的基础设施。更重要的是，论文明确总结出一个经验：agent 能力往往通过 hierarchical optimization 学得更好，而不是 monolithic end-to-end training。这意味着他们实际训练中很可能把感知、工具使用、规划、执行拆成不同层次或不同阶段优化，而不是只用统一 reward 直接推全链路。23:T404,For a pretraining-oriented reader, the value is not the benchmark spread by itself. The value is that the paper lays out a concrete route toward a native multimodal foundation model. It touches the full stack: visual representation learning, cross-modal alignment, language-model objective design, the pretrain-to-posttrain interface, and multimodal RL infrastructure. Three parts are especially relevant. First, the vision side is not treated as a frozen external encoder; it is pretrained with a deliberate two-stage recipe that separates representation learning from alignment. Second, the paper extends multi-token prediction into the multimodal setting and explicitly studies how image tokens should enter the MTP head. That is a training-objective question, not just a systems trick. Third, the report argues that agentic capability is learned more effectively through hierarchical optimization than through monolithic end-to-end training, which is directly relevant to how pretraining and post-training should be coupled.24:Tbe9,There are four main technical components. First, CogViT, a parameter-efficient vision encoder trained in two stages. Stage 1 uses distillation-based masked image modeling. A student ViT reconstructs masked regions in the feature spaces of two teachers: SigLIP2 for semantic representations and DINOv3 for texture and local structure. The setup uses a 35% masking ratio at 224x224 resolution, with a quality-aware data mixture of 80% high-quality natural images, 10% instruction-following data, and 10% scientific imagery. The intent is clear: build a strong visual backbone before forcing it into cross-modal alignment. Training uses the Muon optimizer, and QK-Norm is introduced to stabilize attention by mitigating logit explosion. Stage 2 switches to contrastive image-text pretraining with the SigLIP loss. The main upgrades are variable-resolution NaFlex inputs, a 64K global batch for large-scale contrastive learning, and an 8B bilingual Chinese-English image-text corpus. This is a representation-first, alignment-second recipe rather than pure contrastive training from scratch.

Second, MMTP, or Multimodal Multi-Token Prediction. In text-only MTP, prefix tokens can be passed naturally into the MTP head via token embeddings. In multimodal settings, however, it is unclear how image tokens should be exposed to the MTP head. The paper compares three options: directly passing visual embeddings, masking out visual tokens entirely at the MTP head, or preserving visual positional information while replacing visual content with a shared learnable <|image|> token. GLM-5V-Turbo adopts the third option. The reason is the trade-off between optimization and infrastructure efficiency. Direct visual embeddings make the head heavier and potentially less stable; removing visual tokens entirely loses multimodal conditioning; the shared special token preserves the structural signal that image content exists at certain positions without injecting full visual features into the MTP head. This is one of the most interesting parts of the paper for foundation-model training.

Third, multimodal training is deeply integrated across pretraining and supervised fine-tuning, rather than adding vision only at the instruction-tuning stage. The public text does not expose every data-mixture and curriculum detail, but the intended direction is clear: vision should participate in the model’s reasoning backbone, not merely provide a few side tokens.

Fourth, the paper performs joint reinforcement learning over more than 30 task categories spanning perception, reasoning, and agentic behavior, supported by dedicated multimodal RL infrastructure. The more important takeaway is the training philosophy stated in the report: agentic competence is often learned better through hierarchical optimization than through monolithic end-to-end training. That suggests the system likely decomposes perception, tool use, planning, and execution into different optimization layers or stages rather than relying on a single unified reward over the full chain.25:T503,On the vision side, CogViT is reported to achieve competitive results on general object recognition, fine-grained understanding, and geometric or spatial perception, summarized in the paper’s overview figure. On the full model side, GLM-5V-Turbo reports strong results on multimodal agent benchmarks: 30.7 on ImageMining, 51.9 on BrowseComp-VL, 72.9 on MMSearch, and 78.2 on SimpleVQA. On GUI-agent tasks it reaches 75.7 on AndroidWorld and 62.3 on OSWorld. On Claw-based evaluations it reports 87.0/80.7 on PinchBench, 57.7/75.0 on ClawEval, and 57.6 on ZClawBench. For coding, it scores 94.8 on Design2Code, outperforming Claude Opus 4.6, while preserving or improving text-only coding relative to GLM-5-Turbo on CC-Backend, CC-Frontend, and CC-RepoExploration. For a pretraining reader, the most useful evidence is not the absolute benchmark list but the support for specific design choices: the comparison among the three MMTP visual-token interfaces, whether the two-stage CogViT recipe beats direct image-text alignment, and whether joint RL improves broad generalization rather than overfitting a narrow set of agent tasks. If the full report includes ablations, the most informative ones are likely around Muon, QK-Norm, NaFlex, SigLIP loss, and the large bilingual corpus.26:T4dd,Read the model, training, and infrastructure section first. For deeper reading, keep three questions in mind: whether MMTP improves sample efficiency or generation behavior in multimodal settings, whether stronger standalone vision pretraining creates alignment tension with the language backbone, and how much of the RL gain comes from broader task coverage versus better reward and verification design.27:T65c,The pipeline has three stages. First, video filtering and segmentation. The system identifies instructional videos and splits each long video into visually coherent shots. This is not just a preprocessing convenience. It creates local contexts where a single action or sub-action is more likely to dominate, which makes later grounding more reliable. The pipeline also filters segments with poor visual-text alignment to avoid forcing the model to explain mismatched evidence. Second, candidate step generation. Qwen2.5-VL-72B is used to jointly inspect frames and transcripts and produce candidate procedural steps for each shot. The target is not generic captioning. It is structured procedural abstraction: converting noisy narration plus visual evidence into concise, instruction-like steps tied to temporal spans. Third, refinement and merging. DeepSeek-R1 is then used to clean up semantics, resolve vague references, improve temporal alignment, and merge fragmented steps across neighboring shots when they belong to the same action. This division of labor is the key design choice. The VLM handles local perception and grounding; the reasoning LLM handles long-context cleanup, rewriting, and global consistency. Compared with earlier pipelines that relied on a single model over transcripts or coarse visual cues, this design explicitly separates local multimodal evidence extraction from global temporal-semantic consolidation. The paper also introduces DenseCaption100, a human-annotated benchmark of 100 instructional videos, to validate annotation quality directly rather than relying only on downstream task gains.28:T4a3,On the data side, the pipeline produces DenseStep2M with about 100K videos and 2M fine-grained procedural steps. On the validation side, the paper uses DenseCaption100 to compare automated annotations against human-written dense annotations. The abstract does not expose the full metric table, but the paper’s central claim is that the generated annotations align well with human standards in both semantics and temporal grounding. On the model side, fine-tuning VLMs on DenseStep2M improves both generative tasks, such as dense captioning and procedural grounding, and discriminative tasks, such as cross-modal retrieval. That pattern matters. If the pipeline only improved linguistic fluency, one would expect gains mostly on captioning. Gains on grounding and retrieval suggest that temporal alignment quality also improved. The most informative ablations are likely the ones removing shot segmentation, alignment filtering, or the DeepSeek-R1 refinement stage. These components correspond to local context stabilization, bad-sample removal, and global semantic-temporal correction, respectively, so they are the natural places to inspect where the quality gains actually come from.29:T42a,Start with the introduction and focus on the failure mode of using raw ASR supervision for long-horizon video learning. Then read the pipeline figure and the sections on segmentation, candidate generation, and refinement. If reading time is limited, the pipeline overview, the annotation quality table, and the ablations on filtering/refinement are enough to capture the paper’s value in about 20 to 30 minutes. For a pretraining reader, two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: whether the gains come more from text normalization or from better temporal alignment, and whether the same relabeling recipe can be transferred to other long-sequence multimodal data such as speech, GUI trajectories, or web interaction traces.2a:T77e,The method has three main components. First, transition construction. Starting from an anchor observation s_t, the framework samples a parameterized egocentric action a_t and uses a controllable world model to synthesize a future view s_{t+1}^{WM}. The teacher is intentionally modular; the experiments instantiate it with Stable Virtual Camera and HY-WorldPlay to test whether the supervision depends on a specific generator. The key point is not image generation alone, but the creation of geometrically aligned source-action-target triples with explicit motion semantics.

Second, bidirectional spatial supervision. Instead of training only on forward prediction, the paper builds both forward and inverse tasks. Forward spatial reasoning asks the model to infer the consequence of an action given the current observation. Inverse spatial reasoning asks it to recover the action from the before/after views. This is a sensible design choice: dynamic spatial competence requires both identifying the latent transition and propagating it to unseen future states. Compared with generic VQA-style supervision, this is much closer to learning state transitions.

Third, a two-stage post-training recipe. The model is first trained with SFT on the structured spatial tasks, then refined with task-aware GRPO. The role split is clear: SFT transfers the teacher signal into the model, while GRPO sharpens structured outputs and decision boundaries for action-conditioned reasoning. The paper does not present RL as a generic add-on; it is used to improve correctness on spatially grounded outputs.

A notable design choice is the deliberate avoidance of test-time coupling. The authors are not arguing that world models are unnecessary; rather, they move the world-model benefit into training so deployment remains a standard single-model VLM inference pipeline. That trade-off is important for scalable multimodal systems.2b:T644,The evaluation covers SAT-Real, SAT-Synthesized, VSI-Bench, and MindCube, all of which contain substantial viewpoint-conditioned or dynamic spatial reasoning components. The main empirical claim is consistent improvement over the base Qwen2.5-VL model on all four benchmarks. The GRPO stage further improves over the SFT checkpoint. The paper also compares against a MindJourney-style test-time world-model-coupled baseline and reports that the distilled standalone VLM is both more effective and cheaper at deployment.

The most informative experimental axes are threefold. First, teacher ablations: different world models are used as teachers, suggesting the gains are not tied to one specific generator. Second, training-recipe ablations: base model vs SFT-only vs SFT→GRPO, which isolates the contribution of each stage. Third, fine-grained category analysis on SAT-Real: the gains are concentrated on egocentric movement, action consequence, and perspective taking, which supports the claim that the method improves motion-conditioned reasoning rather than static recognition.

Start with the introduction, especially the argument against the two common alternatives: static data scaling without explicit transition modeling, and inference-time world-model coupling with high runtime overhead. That framing explains why the method is built the way it is.

Then move to Figure 2 and the transition-construction section. This is the core of the paper. The important question is how anchor observations, camera trajectories, and world-model generations are turned into structured supervision rather than mere data augmentation.

In the experiments, prioritize the main benchmark table, teacher replacement ablations, SFT vs GRPO ablations, and the fine-grained SAT-Real analysis. Around 30–45 minutes is enough to capture the main idea. If the goal is to assess transferability to native multimodal pretraining, spend extra time on the appendix sections covering data construction and prompting templates.2d:T812,The method combines a theoretical analysis of entropy dynamics with a simple rejection-sampling controller. The theory starts from a simplified policy-gradient objective without clipping or importance weighting and studies how predictive entropy changes after an update. The key result is that entropy change is negatively related to advantage, and the effect is amplified when the model is already highly confident. Intuitively, positive-advantage samples reinforce selected tokens, sharpen the distribution, and reduce entropy; negative-advantage samples do the opposite and tend to flatten the distribution. In high-confidence regions, this sharpening or flattening effect becomes stronger, which explains why entropy collapse can become self-reinforcing once the policy has already narrowed.

Entrocraft uses this result operationally. Instead of adding entropy regularization to the loss, it performs rejection sampling on rollout samples before the policy update. If the observed entropy is below the target schedule, it preferentially keeps samples whose advantages push entropy upward and filters out samples that would reduce entropy further. If entropy is above target, it does the reverse. In effect, it biases the advantage distribution so that the resulting gradient steers the policy toward the desired entropy level. Because the intervention happens directly at the sample-distribution level, the controller reacts quickly, often within a few update steps, rather than relying on a weakly coupled regularization term. The paper emphasizes two practical properties: it is drop-in for existing RL pipelines, and it is advantage-estimator-agnostic.

A notable aspect is that the paper is about crafting an entropy curve, not merely preserving entropy. It treats entropy scheduling similarly to learning-rate scheduling and compares different schedule families. The main empirical conclusion is that linear entropy annealing works best: keep exploration sufficiently high early on, then gradually tighten the policy later for stability and generalization.2e:T664,The experimental story is centered on long-horizon RL rather than short-run reward gains. The paper reports that Entrocraft extends the useful training horizon by up to 4× before performance plateaus, which directly supports the anti-saturation claim. It also reports a strong generalization result: a 4B model with Entrocraft surpasses an 8B baseline. On diversity-sensitive evaluation, pass@K improves by about 50% over the baseline, consistent with the claim that better entropy control improves exploration coverage rather than merely sharpening one preferred trajectory.

From the paper structure and the introduction, the key experiments likely include four layers. First, entropy-curve tracking: regularization- and clipping-based baselines may raise entropy, but their curves drift or oscillate over long training, while Entrocraft follows the target schedule more closely. Second, schedule ablations: constant entropy, increasing schedules, decreasing schedules, and linear annealing are compared, with linear annealing performing best. Third, compatibility across RL algorithms: the method is presented as a drop-in and advantage-estimator-agnostic, so at least GRPO/GSPO-style settings are likely covered. Fourth, empirical validation of the theory: entropy collapse is more severe in high-confidence regions, and biasing the advantage distribution changes subsequent entropy dynamics in the predicted direction. The full benchmark tables are not visible in the provided excerpt, but the headline numbers—4B beating an 8B baseline, pass@K +50%, and 4× longer improvement horizon—already capture the scale of the effect.2f:T673,This one deserves a careful read. Start with the introduction to understand the exact target: the paper is not arguing that prior methods fail to preserve entropy at all, but that they fail to control it precisely enough for long-run RL. Then read the theory section closely. Two statements matter most: entropy change is tied to the sign of advantage, and high confidence amplifies entropy change. Those results are the backbone of the method; without them, the rejection-sampling controller would look heuristic.

Next, move to the algorithm section and inspect the filtering rule in detail: when are positive-advantage samples rejected, when are negative-advantage samples rejected, what entropy statistic is tracked, and how does the controller avoid over-correction? In the experiments, prioritize three kinds of figures and tables. First, entropy-curve alignment plots, to verify that “precise control” is real. Second, long-run training curves, to see whether the method truly delays saturation rather than only improving early learning speed. Third, schedule ablations, especially the evidence for linear annealing over constant high entropy. If time is limited, the theory proposition, the algorithm pseudocode, the main results table, and the schedule ablation figure are the minimum set. For deeper study, three follow-up questions are worth pursuing: whether the sample filtering introduces systematic advantage bias or reward hacking, how this differs from KL-to-reference or temperature-based control, and whether entropy scheduling could be moved earlier into mid-training or RL pretraining as a more general exploration curriculum.30:T4f1,Pipeline:
1) Collect model-native CoT traces by sampling reasoning outputs from the target model on OpenThoughts3 prompts.
2) Entropy-based framing: compute per-token conditional entropy H_θ(t_i|t_<i). The paper argues reasoning tokens split into (a) structural tokens—recurring multi-token phrases whose continuation becomes near-deterministic once started (low entropy), and (b) organic tokens—problem-specific content (math expressions, bindings, intermediate results) with higher entropy. They derive a compression ceiling showing that if the mean entropy inside merge spans is much smaller than log|V|, the achievable compression is approximately the fraction of tokens covered by merges.
3) Learn “reasoning supertokens” via cross-word BPE (SuperBPE) on the reasoning traces only, append them to the base vocabulary, and teach the model to use them via SFT. To limit distribution skew, n-gram counts are capped per sample. Training updates embeddings, LM head, and only a small subset of transformer layers to minimize disruption.

Design choice: compress the surface form (tokenization of structural phrases) rather than the reasoning content, preserving full readability; supertokens also act as explicit, human-readable markers of reasoning moves.31:T400,DORA introduces multi-version streaming training. Rollout workers host multiple policy versions concurrently and stream completed trajectories to training without a batch-level barrier. The design enforces three constraints: C1, each trajectory is generated entirely by one policy version; C2, trajectories are retained rather than dropped, including long-tail CoT samples; and C3, a sliding window bounds staleness between rollout and training policies. A centralized load-balancing orchestrator reallocates resources across policy versions and migrates requests to keep rollout workers utilized. Preserving C1 also enables an important systems optimization: KV-cache states are equivalent across workers hosting the same policy version, so migration can avoid re-prefill. This differs from partial-rollout methods, which split long trajectories across weight updates and reduce waiting time at the cost of relaxing intra-trajectory consistency and adding re-prefill overhead, especially under MoE or long-context settings.32:T6b3,PAINT has two coupled components.

The first is partial-solution adaptive masking. Instead of always exposing the full verified solution or using a fixed masking ratio, the method adapts the amount of visible solution context based on rollout–reference overlap. If the student rollout is far from the reference, the privileged branch sees more of the solution, producing stronger corrective guidance. If the rollout is already close, the method hides more of the reference to preserve diversity and avoid forcing one canonical trace. This directly controls the sharpness-versus-diversity trade-off in the privileged target distribution.

The second is sparse teacher energy interpolation. The method compares the base view and the privileged view on the same student-generated prefixes, then ranks token positions using an entropy-ratio-style statistic that captures where the privileged context changes local uncertainty the most. Only those selected mismatch positions receive interpolated teacher targets before applying the distillation loss. The key idea is not to replace the student target everywhere, but to inject teacher guidance sparsely and locally at decision-relevant positions.

Conceptually, the paper moves privileged self-distillation away from “give the teacher more information and distill the whole sequence” toward “use extra context to locally re-score the student’s own states, then distill only where that re-scoring is informative.” Compared with off-policy distillation, the prefixes are on-policy. Compared with GRPO/RLVR, the supervision is dense and token-level. Compared with external-teacher distillation, the teacher is simply another conditional view of the same model.33:T612,The experiments cover Qwen3-8B, 4B, and 1.7B on three competition-level math benchmarks. The headline result reported in the text is consistent gains over the prior on-policy self-distillation baseline, with macro Avg@12 improvements of 0.8 to 2.1 points. The method also matches or exceeds GRPO across scales while using short single-rollout training instead of grouped long rollouts, leading to better rollout-token efficiency.

The most informative findings are in the ablations. Full-solution exposure is not always optimal; partial exposure is often better, supporting the claim that too much privileged information can collapse the target toward a single trace. Adaptive masking outperforms fixed masking, suggesting that rollout–reference overlap is a useful alignment proxy. Sparse interpolation beats uniform distillation across all positions, which supports the paper’s claim that supervision should concentrate on mathematically consequential tokens rather than stylistic continuation. Relative to SFT, the gains appear to come from on-policy prefix alignment rather than merely having reference traces. Relative to GRPO, the method offers a better efficiency trade-off by replacing some high-variance sequence-level credit assignment with dense local supervision.

Those should be checked directly in the main results and ablation tables.34:T429,Start with the introduction and the main method figure. The two ideas to keep in mind are simple: more privileged context is not always better, and denser teacher supervision is not always better. Then read the method section carefully for the exact definitions of rollout–reference overlap, the entropy-ratio statistic, and how interpolation is applied—on logits or distributions, with thresholding or top-k selection.

In the experiments, prioritize the main results table, the masking ablations, and the token-position selection ablations. For a skim-level read, 30–40 minutes is enough to extract the main contribution. If reading more deeply, three questions are worth following. Can the contextual re-scoring view generalize to other privileged signals such as tool traces, retrieved evidence, or execution states? Is overlap too surface-form-dependent when multiple valid reasoning paths exist? And is entropy mismatch only a heuristic, or could it be replaced by a more principled local credit signal derived from a verifier or an advantage estimator?35:T988,方法由四段组成。第一段是 hidden evidence extraction。给定问题、答案以及 D-LLM 的去噪轨迹，作者并不保留所有 token、所有 step、所有 layer 的隐藏状态，而是先做位置筛选：每个保留的去噪步只看两类位置，一类是当前答案序列的最后一个 token 位置，另一类是相对前一步发生 token identity 变化的位置。这个设计很有针对性，因为 diffusion 文本生成里真正反映“答案正在被修正或定型”的位置，往往集中在这些 changed positions。第二段是压缩。原始 hidden state 维度太高，直接送入 verifier 成本很大，所以作者对每层 hidden state 做低维随机投影，文中用的是 OPORP 风格的 random projection，把 d 压到 r，形成每个 step-layer 的 candidate evidence unit。这里的取舍很明确：他们不要重训练一个复杂的 evidence encoder，而是用近似保距的轻量压缩保留相对结构信息。

第三段是 step-layer evidence selection。候选证据仍然很多，所以作者训练一个选择器，对每个 step-layer 单元打分，在固定 evidence budget 下保留 top-K。关键点在于选择粒度是“step-layer”，不是简单地对整条轨迹做池化，也不是只选 step 不选 layer。作者的核心假设是：幻觉相关信号既有时间稀疏性，也有层级稀疏性。某些层更像语义定型层，某些步更像错误固化步，混在一起平均会把信号冲淡。

第四段是 evidence-conditioned verification。被选中的证据不会直接拼成文本，而是通过 prefix embeddings 注入到 verifier language model。verifier 接收原始 QA 对以及这些前缀证据，输出结构化验证结果，包括是否幻觉、幻觉类型、支持/反驳证据对、简短 rationale。与此同时，作者不只取离散标签，还把 verifier 决策 token 的 logits 映射成连续 hallucination score。这样做的好处是兼顾分类和排序：既能做 binary detection，也能做 AUROC/AUPRC 这类连续评估。

和已有方法相比，区别主要有三点。其一，不再依赖 output-only uncertainty 或 self-consistency，而是显式利用 denoising trajectory。其二，不满足于轨迹级统计量，而是做可选择的 hidden evidence 单元。其三，把检测器设计成 verifier LM，而不是单纯的二分类头，因此可以自然扩展到结构化解释输出。36:T89f,HIVE has four main stages. First, it extracts candidate hidden evidence from the denoising trajectory. The method does not keep all token positions from all steps and layers. Instead, for each retained denoising step, it focuses on the last token position of the current answer and on positions whose decoded token identities changed relative to the previous step. This is a targeted design: in diffusion text generation, token changes often mark where the answer is still being revised or where an error is getting locked in.

Second, it compresses hidden states. Since raw hidden vectors are too large to feed directly into a verifier, the method applies low-dimensional random projections, using an OPORP-style projection, to map each hidden state from dimension d to a much smaller evidence dimension r. This avoids training a heavy evidence encoder while preserving enough geometry for downstream selection.

Third, it performs step-layer evidence selection. Each retained step-layer pair forms a candidate evidence unit. A selector scores these units and keeps only the top-K under a fixed evidence budget. This is the key structural choice. The selection is not only temporal and not only layerwise; it is explicitly step-by-layer. The underlying hypothesis is that hallucination signals are sparse both across denoising time and across network depth. Pooling everything together would wash out the useful signal.

Fourth, the selected evidence is injected into a verifier LM through prefix embeddings. The verifier takes the QA pair together with the evidence prefixes and produces structured verification outputs: hallucination decision, hallucination type, evidence pairs, and short rationales. In parallel, the logits associated with the verifier’s decision tokens are converted into a continuous hallucination score. This gives both a scalar detector and a structured verifier.

Relative to prior work, the differences are clear. It uses denoising trajectories rather than output-only uncertainty, it selects explicit hidden evidence rather than relying on coarse trace summaries, and it uses an LM verifier instead of a simple classification head, which naturally supports structured outputs.37:T5f9,The evaluation covers two diffusion LLMs, three QA benchmarks, and eight baselines. The main empirical result is consistent: HIVE outperforms output-only uncertainty methods, trajectory-summary baselines, and variants without explicit step-layer evidence selection. The paper emphasizes detection metrics such as AUROC and AUPRC, along with the quality of structured verification outputs.

The ablations are more informative than the headline table. First, evidence-source ablations show that using only final-answer hidden states is weaker than using denoising trajectories, which supports the claim that hallucination signals are distributed across the generation process. Second, selector ablations show that removing the step-layer selector, replacing it with averaging, or using random evidence hurts performance. This directly supports the sparse-evidence hypothesis. Third, evidence-injection ablations indicate that prefix conditioning works better than cruder alternatives, suggesting that the verifier benefits from structured access to hidden evidence. Fourth, budget and retained-step studies show that the method remains effective under a limited evidence budget, so the gains are not simply coming from feeding much more information.

For a pretraining-oriented reader, the most useful takeaway is not the exact margin over baselines, but the experimental confirmation that intermediate denoising states contain stable error signals and that these signals become more useful when selected at step-layer granularity.38:T4b4,The method has two layers. The first is High Entropy Phase (HEP), a variable-length segment of consecutive high-entropy tokens. Instead of averaging entropy over fixed windows, the paper detects bursts: a phase starts when token entropy crosses a high threshold and ends only after a specified number of consecutive tokens fall below a low threshold. This is meant to smooth token-level noise and better align the signal with a coherent reasoning unit under uncertainty. The second layer is the Entropy Centroid. Each HEP is assigned a mass equal to its token length and a position equal to its midpoint in the trajectory. The centroid is then the weighted average position of all HEPs in the trajectory. At selection time, among N sampled trajectories, the method simply chooses the one with the lowest centroid. The intuition is that good trajectories tend to explore early and settle later, whereas bad trajectories remain uncertain late into generation. Compared with prior intrinsic signals such as mean entropy, confidence, KL, or fixed-window aggregation, this method explicitly encodes temporal placement of uncertainty. Compared with reward-model-based selection, it adds no extra training cost.39:T43c,The evaluation spans models from 14B to 480B parameters and covers mathematics, code generation, logical reasoning, and agentic tasks. The main empirical claim is consistency: Lowest Centroid outperforms existing intrinsic baselines across settings, and the gains remain stable as model size increases. The analysis section appears to be more important than any single benchmark number. The paper shows that high-entropy tokens indeed occur in bursts rather than as isolated spikes, which justifies moving from tokens to phases. It also shows a separation between correct and incorrect trajectories in centroid space: correct trajectories concentrate uncertainty earlier, while incorrect ones exhibit more late-stage confusion. Relative to token-level entropy, confidence, KL-based signals, and fixed-window averages, the centroid better distinguishes useful exploration from unresolved uncertainty. From the provided full-text excerpt, the exact benchmark tables are not fully visible, so the main takeaway is robustness across tasks and scales rather than one standout numeric gain.3a:T812,DreamProver is built around an iterative wake-sleep cycle.

In the wake stage, the system attempts to prove training theorems using the current lemma library. Two mechanisms are central. The first is learnable theorem identification. Under a fixed budget of proof attempts and error-correction attempts, the LLM is prompted to prove a target theorem using the current library. If it succeeds within budget, the theorem is treated as learnable at the current knowledge level and may imply a more advanced reusable lemma built on top of existing ones. The second is intermediate theorem discovery. When a theorem is too hard to prove directly, the system recursively decomposes it into more tractable intermediate theorems. These intermediate theorems become the raw material for later lemma induction.

In the sleep stage, the system does not simply append all discovered lemmas to the library. Instead, it performs structured lemma evolution. Based on the method description, this includes clustering, pruning, abstraction, and validation: semantically similar candidate lemmas are grouped and deduplicated; each cluster is abstracted into a more general and reusable lemma; the resulting abstractions are formally validated; and the library is updated with a compact set of accepted lemmas. The design trade-off is important. If the system optimizes only for coverage, the library becomes a bag of local tricks. If it optimizes only for abstraction, it risks producing overly general or unverifiable lemmas. DreamProver tries to balance provability, compactness, and transferability.

At test time, the learned lemma library is used explicitly to prove unseen theorems. So the learned artifact is not just an updated model state, but an externalized, compressed set of formal skills. This distinguishes it from methods that repeatedly synthesize fresh intermediate lemmas online.

Relative to prior work, DreamProver is neither fixed-library retrieval nor per-problem lemma synthesis. Its novelty is the closed loop of discovery, abstraction, validation, and reuse.3b:T6ee,The experiments span multiple formal math domains, including inequality, number theory, combinatorics, plane geometry, and machine learning theory. The headline result reported by the authors is a 61% improvement in proving success rate when using the learned lemma library, along with a 50% reduction in proof length and a 48% reduction in token usage. This suggests the gains are not only about solving more problems, but also about finding shorter and cheaper proof trajectories.

From the available full-text content, the most informative experimental dimensions appear to be the following. First, cross-domain effectiveness: the method is evaluated on several mathematical domains rather than a single narrow benchmark, which supports the claim that the learned lemmas are transferable rather than purely local heuristics. Second, library-quality analysis: the paper emphasizes that the resulting library is compact, so the experiments likely include statistics on library size, reuse frequency, and the effect of clustering/abstraction. Third, compute efficiency: the reductions in proof length and token usage indicate that the learned lemmas improve search structure rather than merely benefiting from larger sampling budgets.

The provided excerpt does not include the full result tables or all baseline names, so it would be unsafe to invent exact per-benchmark success rates, library sizes, or iteration counts. The reliable quantitative claims are the three numbers stated explicitly in the abstract: +61% proving success, -50% proof length, and -48% token usage. A careful read should verify the main result table, the wake-sleep iteration curve, the library-size and reuse analysis, and ablations that remove sleep-stage abstraction, clustering, or validation.3c:T562,Read this paper as a study of knowledge accumulation rather than just another proving pipeline.

Start with the introduction and focus on the two limitations it targets: fixed lemma libraries and problem-specific intermediate lemmas. Once that contrast is clear, the wake-sleep design becomes much easier to evaluate.

Then read the wake stage carefully. The important details are how learnable theorems are identified, how the proof budget is defined, and how failure triggers recursive decomposition. These choices determine whether the collected candidates are useful signals or mostly noise.

Next, spend most of the time on the sleep stage. This is where the real contribution lies. Look at how clustering and pruning are implemented, what representation is used for semantic similarity, how abstraction is prompted or programmatically constrained, how formal validation is performed, and how the library is updated over iterations.

In the experiments, prioritize the main results, the performance-vs-iteration curve, and the library-quality analysis. If time is limited, about 30 minutes is enough to capture the main mechanism. If the goal is to judge whether the method generalizes to broader self-improving reasoning agents, the appendix and failure cases are worth reading, especially examples of over-abstraction, unverifiable lemmas, or uncontrolled library growth.3d:T8d0,核心方法是 bilevel optimization。外层优化 workflow sketch，内层优化固定 sketch 下每个 LLM call 的 prompt。workflow 被表示成一串文本化的高层调用描述 a1 到 aK，每个调用对应一个更细的 prompt θi。高层 sketch 只描述“这一步做什么”，低层 prompt 决定“具体怎么做、用什么工具、如何措辞”。这种分离很重要，因为如果直接在完整 prompt 空间里同时搜索结构和参数，搜索空间会过大且难以稳定。

内层优化采用 layer-wise textual backpropagation。作者把 workflow 看成模块堆叠：输入 x0 经过第 i 个调用得到 xi。先做一次前向执行，得到所有中间输出。然后对最后一层，用一个专门的 LLM 根据最终输出、参考答案和损失，生成针对最后一个模块的 textual gradient。这个 gradient 不是数值向量，而是一段自然语言反馈，描述该模块应该如何调整输出行为。对中间层，则再用另一个 LLM，根据当前层输入、当前层输出以及下一层传回的 textual gradient，生成当前层的 textual gradient。这样就形成了从后往前的语言版误差信号传播。最后，再用优化器 LLM 汇总一个 batch 上的 textual gradients，改写对应模块的 prompt。

外层优化则修改 workflow sketch 本身。作者同样使用 textual gradients，但对象从 prompt 变成结构描述：可以删除已有调用、插入新调用、调整调用顺序或职责分工。这里的设计借鉴 NAS，但没有把结构离散化成固定算子集合，而是直接在文本空间里编辑 workflow 描述。优点是表达能力强，能自然容纳 retrieval、planning、verification、tool use 等异构步骤；代价是搜索稳定性更依赖底层 LLM 的判断质量。

和已有 prompt optimization 方法相比，FlowBot 的区别在于它不假设 workflow 结构固定。和自动 workflow generation 方法相比，它又不是一次性生成后就结束，而是通过内外两层交替更新，让结构和节点提示共同演化。作者还强调 modular optimization：每个模块的更新要显式条件于下游模块的错误，这比独立优化单步 prompt 更接近真正的组合系统调优。3e:T893,The method is a bilevel optimization framework. The outer loop optimizes a workflow sketch, while the inner loop optimizes the prompts of individual LLM calls under a fixed sketch. A workflow is represented as a sequence of high-level textual call descriptions a1 to aK, and each call has an associated detailed prompt theta_i. The sketch specifies what each step is supposed to do, while the prompt specifies how it should do it, including instructions and tool access. This separation keeps the search space manageable.

The inner loop performs layer-wise textual backpropagation. The workflow is treated like a stack of modules: x0 is the input, and each call maps x_{i-1} to x_i. After a forward pass, the final module receives a textual gradient generated by another LLM conditioned on the final output, the gold answer, and the task loss. This gradient is not numeric; it is natural-language feedback describing how the module should change its behavior. For intermediate modules, another LLM generates a textual gradient based on the current module’s input, output, and the downstream gradient. In this way, error information is propagated backward through the workflow in language form. A separate optimizer LLM then aggregates gradients over a batch and rewrites the module prompt.

The outer loop edits the workflow sketch itself. It uses textual feedback to add calls, delete calls, and revise the decomposition of responsibilities across steps. This is inspired by neural architecture search, but instead of searching over a fixed operator library, it edits workflow descriptions directly in text space. That makes the method expressive enough to represent retrieval, planning, verification, and tool use in a unified way, though it also makes optimization more dependent on the quality of the underlying LLM.

Compared with prior prompt-optimization methods, FlowBot does not assume a fixed workflow. Compared with one-shot workflow generation methods, it alternates between structure updates and module-level prompt updates, allowing both to co-evolve. The modular optimization aspect is important: each module is updated in light of downstream failures rather than in isolation.3f:T539,The empirical study compares FlowBot against two classes of baselines: prompt-optimization methods on human-crafted workflows, such as GEPA, and automated workflow-generation methods, such as AFlow. The main result is that FlowBot is competitive across multiple tasks and tends to discover sensible workflow decompositions rather than arbitrary chains of calls.

The ablations carry most of the weight. Removing the outer-loop structural search and keeping only prompt optimization under a fixed workflow hurts performance, which shows that structure search matters. Removing the inner-loop modular textual backprop and replacing it with coarser updates also hurts, which supports the claim that layer-wise textual gradients provide more targeted optimization signals. The paper also includes qualitative evidence that induced workflows often separate responsibilities in a meaningful way, for example decomposing a task into planning, retrieval or tool use, and final synthesis or verification.

The provided text does not include the full numerical tables, task list, or model sizes, so it is not possible to reproduce exact gains here without overclaiming. What is clear is that the evaluation is built around multi-task comparisons and two critical ablation axes, which is the right evidence structure for this kind of systems Uniform-based Discrete Diffusion Models 解释为一种 associative memory，并用 token recovery 和 conditional entropy 来量化训练样本与测试样本周围的吸引盆。","importance":"这是 diffusion LM 方向里比较值得精读的一类工作。贡献不在于提出更强的模型，而在于给离散扩散语言模型提供了一个可操作的机制解释和诊断框架。AR LM 的记忆与泛化通常通过抽取攻击、n-gram overlap 或下游指标间接观察；这篇论文把问题改写成反向去噪动力学中的稳定点和吸引盆问题。对预训练研究的启发有三点。第一，数据规模不只是降低验证 loss，也会改变模型的吸引盆结构：训练样本的局部盆会收缩，而未见样本附近的盆会扩展。第二，conditional entropy 可以作为 token 稳定性的探针，比只看生成样本是否重复训练文本更细。第三，它说明离散 diffusion LM 的“泛化”可以表现为对未见但来自同一数据分布的序列进行恢复，而不是简单地从噪声中生成新文本。","technical_approach":"论文聚焦 UDDM，即以 uniform corruption 为前向噪声过程的离散扩散模型。模型在反向过程中学习条件分布，用局部条件似然来预测被破坏 token。作者把这种条件似然最大化与 associative memory 联系起来：传统 Hopfield network 依赖显式 energy function 来定义稳定吸引子，但 feed-forward Transformer 一般没有对称权重结构，也不适合强行写成能量模型。论文的关键转向是放弃显式 energy 要求，改用 pseudo-likelihood / conditional likelihood 的动力学视角。简化分析表明，最大化条件似然会隐式产生类似 Hebbian learning 的效应，同时扩大分类 margin，从而在数据点附近形成吸引盆。这个桥接很重要，因为它把离散扩散 LM 的训练目标和记忆系统的恢复动力学接在了一起。方法上，论文用两类探针观察这个结构：token recovery rate 测量被噪声破坏后是否能恢复原 token；conditional entropy 测量模型在给定上下文和扩散步时对 token 的不确定性。低 entropy 且恢复成功的 token 对应更稳定的局部吸引结构。","experiments":"实验围绕训练集大小驱动的 memorization-to-generalization transition 展开。小训练集阶段，模型能较可靠地恢复训练样本 token，但对测试样本 token 不稳定，经常在反向扩散中改写未见序列。随着训练样本数增加，训练点周围的吸引盆不再无限扩张，反而逐渐收缩；同时，来自同一数据分布的测试序列周围开始出现局部稳定区域。进入泛化区间后，模型不仅能保留未被破坏的测试 token，也能从部分破坏的测试序列中恢复 token。论文用 token-level conditional entropy 解释不同恢复行为：成功恢复的 token 通常接近零熵，失败或不稳定 token 的 entropy 更高。sequence-level conditional entropy 则用于检测整体转变；当进入泛化区间后，训练序列与合成序列的 entropy 分布趋于对齐。给出的正文片段没有包含具体模型参数量、语料规模和精确数值表，因此关键应读作趋势和机制证据，而不是可直接外推到 frontier-scale 模型的 scaling law。","reading_guide":"建议精读。先看 Introduction 中对 associative memory、memory blackout、generalization phase 的定义，避免把“retrieving unseen data”误读成隐私泄漏意义上的训练集记忆。再看理论部分，重点是 conditional likelihood 为什么足以形成吸引盆，以及它如何在简化设定中对应 Hebbian learning 和 margin maximization。实验部分重点看 Fig. 1、Fig. 2 和 Fig. 3：这些图展示训练样本与测试样本 token recovery 随训练集大小变化的差异。Fig. 4 的 sequence-level conditional entropy 更适合当作诊断指标来读。值得深挖的问题有两个：一是这个 AM 视角能否解释 MDLM、SEDD、LLaDA 等不同 mask/noise schedule 下的差异；二是 conditional entropy 能否在大规模 diffusion LM 训练中作为数据重复、过拟合或泛化相变的在线监控信号。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies when discrete language diffusion models memorize training examples and when they enter a regime where unseen samples from the same data distribution become stable under the reverse denoising dynamics. It frames Uniform-based Discrete Diffusion Models as associative memories and measures the basin structure through token recovery and conditional entropy.","importance":"This is a useful paper for researchers following non-autoregressive language modeling. Its main contribution is not a better model recipe, but a mechanistic and measurable account of memorization and generalization in discrete diffusion LMs. The associative-memory view turns a vague question, whether the model memorizes or generalizes, into a question about attractors, basins, and token stability. For pretraining, the important implication is that increasing data size can change the geometry of retrieval: basins around individual training examples shrink, while partial basins around unseen but in-distribution examples expand. This gives a more precise lens for studying data scale, memorization, and generalization in diffusion-style language models.","technical_approach":"$16","experiments":"$17","reading_guide":"Read it carefully if you work on diffusion LMs, masked language modeling, or memorization diagnostics. timestep。低噪声时教师看到更多上下文，预测更可靠；高噪声时教师更接近猜测，因此蒸馏权重需要随 timestep 调整。同时，学生在训练早期和后期吸收软标签的能力不同，所以还要随训练阶段调整蒸馏强度。CompDemo 解决高 mask ratio 下教师上下文不足的问题。它使用 complementary mask splitting，让教师在互补的 mask 视图上产生更有信息量的 cross-architecture distillation for diffusion LLMs: transferring knowledge from a large dLLM teacher to a small dLLM student when the two models may differ in backbone architecture, attention pattern, and tokenizer. The difficulty is specific to diffusion LMs. Teacher reliability changes with the diffusion timestep, heavily masked inputs provide weak context, and mismatched vocabularies make standard token-level distillation objectives ill-defined.","importance":"The main value for pretraining research is that it treats dLLM compression as heterogeneous knowledge transfer, not just step reduction within the same model family. This matters as large dLLMs such as LLaDA-style models become expensive to train and deploy. The paper also makes a useful modeling point: soft targets in diffusion LMs should not be trusted uniformly across timesteps, because the teacher’s prediction quality depends strongly on the noise level.","technical_approach":"Tide has three modules. Tidal is a dual-axis distillation schedule over training progress and diffusion timestep. The teacher is more reliable at low mask ratios and less reliable at high mask ratios, while the student’s ability to absorb teacher signals also changes during training. The distillation weight is therefore modulated along both axes. CompDemo addresses context starvation under heavy masking. It uses complementary mask splitting to give the teacher richer demonstration-conditioned views, rather than relying on a single highly corrupted input. Reverse Calm addresses tokenizer mismatch. Instead of fragile token-to-token mapping, it uses an inverted chunk-level likelihood matching objective to project teacher knowledge into the student vocabulary space, with bounded gradients and filtering on both ends to reduce noisy alignment. The modules play separate roles: Tidal controls when to distill, CompDemo improves the contextual signal, and Reverse Calm defines how to align outputs across vocabularies.","experiments":"The experiments cover two pipelines. The cross-tokenizer setting distills from a 16B MoE LLaDA2 teacher into a 0.6B BD3LM block-diffusion student. The shared-tokenizer setting distills from an 8B dense WeDLM teacher into the same 0.6B student family. On eight benchmarks, the best configuration improves the average score from 32.67 to 34.20, a gain of +1.53. The distilled dLLM also reports strong code generation behavior, reaching 48.78 on HumanEval versus 32.3 for a same-size AR model. Ablations indicate that the two pipelines prefer different module configurations, which supports the modular design rather than a single fixed recipe.","reading_guide":"Skim in 30–45 minutes. Standard speculative decoding verifies one draft sequence with token-level rejection sampling. SpecTr extends this to multiple drafts and uses optimal transport at each token position, but still verifies positions sequentially. GBV verifies a whole block jointly, but was designed for the single-draft setting. SpecTr-GBV formulates verification as an optimal transport problem between multiple draft token blocks and the target token block. This allows the verifier to exploit both wider draft coverage and block-level acceptance. The paper proves that the resulting sampler preserves the target model distribution. It also proves optimal expected acceptance length within the i.i.d. multi-draft framework for a fixed number of draft sequences, with the optimum increasing as more drafts are used.","experiments":"The paper reports comparisons on five datasets against four baselines, including standard speculative decoding, SpecTr, and GBV. The main metrics are block efficiency and speedup ratio. SpecTr-GBV is reported to outperform the baselines on both metrics by accepting longer blocks and reducing target-model calls. The provided text does not include exact dataset names, model sizes, draft counts, block lengths, or numerical speedup values, so those should be checked in the full experimental tables before citing. The paper also includes hyperparameter ablations to study how settings such as draft count and block length affect acceptance and speed.","reading_guide":"A useful skim takes about 25–40 minutes. Read the introduction carefully for the distinction between SpecTr and GBV; that is the main conceptual setup. Then focus on the optimal-transport formulation in the method section and verify whether the coupling is defined over blocks rather than positions. The proof can be read selectively, with attention to distributional fidelity and optimal expected acceptance length. In the experiments, compare block efficiency with actual wall-clock speedup, since OT overhead, draft-model cost, and target-model batching can change the practical gain.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"做 serving 或推测解码的人可以看；先看理论假设和 wall-clock 表，若 i.i.d. draft 假设太强，这篇更像漂亮上界而不是可直接落地的方法。","en":"Worth a skim if you work on serving or speculative decoding; check the assumptions and wall-clock tables first, because the i.i.d. draft setup may make this more of a clean upper bound than a deployable recipe."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Yijun Lin","Jinhao Sheng","Qingyue Cai","Feng Zhou"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["speculative-decoding","inference-acceleration","block-verification","optimal-transport","serving"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25925","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25925","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2604.26294","title":"Folding Tensor and Sequence Parallelism for Memory-Efficient Transformer Training & Inference","abstract":"We present tensor and sequence parallelism (TSP), a parallel execution strategy that folds tensor parallelism and sequence parallelism onto a single device axis. the dominant memory bottleneck in long-context decoding. Existing eviction policies are largely heuristic (attention-score based or diversity/geometry based). Still useful for foundation-model researchers because (1) it provides a unifying, interpretable objective—mutual-information / capacity maximization—that explains many seemingly different eviction heuristics; (2) for long-context evaluation or continual pretraining setups, eviction policies can confound conclusions about long-range capability by changing what information is effectively retained during decoding. Recommended as a skim focused on the objective and the CapKV approximation.","technical_approach":"The key move is an Information Bottleneck framing under a linear-Gaussian surrogate of attention:\n- Abstraction: treat attention as a linear transmission process and the KV cache entries as a set of linear communication channels.\n- Objective: given a cache budget B, select a subset of channels that maximizes the mutual information between (latent) future queries and the transmitted representations—equivalently, maximize an information-capacity metric of the retained subset.\n- Unification: attention-pattern eviction and structure/diversity-based eviction are interpreted as different approximations to the same global capacity objective.\n- CapKV: a capacity-aware eviction rule that greedily keeps tokens with the highest statistical leverage scores computed from a simplified capacity matrix, yielding a deterministic approximation to the MI-maximization target rather than an empirical scoring heuristic.","experiments":"Reported experiments across multiple model architectures and long-context benchmarks show CapKV achieves a better fidelity–memory trade-off than prior methods, and the derived capacity metric correlates with downstream performance. Unlike static quantization, which applies quantization offline, dynamic quantization operates on tensors at run-time, adapting its parameters to the actual input data. Today's mainstream machine learning frameworks, including ML compilers and inference engines, frequently recommend dynamic quantization as an initial step for optimizing model serving. This is becau","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-04-29T10:10:51+00:00","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2604_26505.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CR","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作指出了一个此前容易被忽略的 serving 安全问题：当多用户请求被合并进同一 batch，并且系统对激活采用跨 batch 的动态量化时，量化参数会由所有样本共同决定，从而形成跨用户的信息侧信道。攻击者只要能与受害者同 batch，并观察自己输出中的细微扰动，就可能反推出受害者输入的内容或属性。","importance":"它对预训练本身没有直接方法贡献，但对大模型推理栈和量化部署有现实影响。原因有两层。第一，动态量化常被当作纯效率优化，默认假设只影响速度、显存和少量精度；这篇工作说明量化粒度还会改变多租户隐私边界。第二，它给出一个很清楚的工程结论：per-token dynamic quantization 不只是精度更稳、实现上更适合 GPU，在安全上也能消除这类跨样本泄漏，而 per-tensor across-batch 的在线量化则存在结构性风险。对做基础设施的人，这比单纯报一个攻击成功率更重要。","technical_approach":"论文分析的漏洞点很具体：某些框架在运行时对激活做动态量化时，会对整个 batch 的激活张量做全局 min/max 归约，得到共享 scale。这样一来，受害者样本的激活幅值会影响攻击者样本被量化到什么网格上，进而影响攻击者前向输出的 logit 或 top-1 log probability。攻击者通过构造输入、反复与目标共置于同一 batch，并测量这些输出扰动，就能把受害者信息从共享量化参数中“读”出来。\n\n方法上，作者没有发明新的量化算法，而是把攻击面拆得很清楚：静态量化不受影响，因为 scale 离线固定；per-token dynamic quantization 也不受影响，因为每个 token 独立计算 scale，不存在跨样本耦合；真正危险的是 per-tensor、尤其是 across-batch 的动态量化。论文还解释了为什么这种模式在工程上仍然存在：例如某些 FP8 online quantization 路径、通用框架的默认 API、以及部分 serving 框架的特定配置。\n\n另一个值得注意的点是，作者不仅从安全角度批评 per-tensor dynamic quantization，也从性能和精度角度补了一刀：在现代 GPU 上，per-token reduction 往往比跨 batch 的全局 reduction 更自然，避免跨 block 同步；而在 LLM 里，不同 token 的激活分布异质性很强，per-token 量化通常也更准确。也就是说，这里不是安全和效率的硬冲突，很多场景下更安全的方案反而也是更合理的默认方案。","experiments":"论文给出的结果相当直接。对 LLM 场景，攻击可以达到 99.6% 到 100% 的 token recovery accuracy；对分类模型，作者展示了 exact image identification。攻击所需能力被刻意压低：只需要与受害者请求同 batch，并观察 top-1 log probabilities，而不需要访问内部激活或梯度。\n\n实验还做了框架层面的漏洞面调查。作者点名了 vLLM 默认的 FP8 online quantization、SGLang 的 per-tensor FP8 模式，以及 ONNX Runtime 和 PyTorch 的动态量化 API 中存在风险的配置。相对地，per-token dynamic quantization 可以完全消除该侧信道。论文也讨论了真实部署中的限制因素，例如硬件与软件异构带来的非确定性、服务提供方可能引入的随机化或调度扰动，以及这些缓解手段与用户期望的 deterministic temperature-0 行为之间的张力。\n\n如果看全文，建议特别关注攻击设置是否要求强同步、是否依赖重复查询、不同 batch size 和不同量化精度下攻击成功率如何变化，以及框架默认配置与可选配置之间的差异。这些细节决定了它是“理论上可行”还是“默认配置下就危险”。从摘要和引言给出的信息看，作者想证明的是后者。","reading_guide":"这篇适合泛读，但值得把方法和威胁模型看清楚。先看 Introduction 和 Background，确认漏洞成立的必要条件：多租户 batching、动态激活量化、量化参数跨 batch 共享。然后直接看攻击方法和主结果表，重点看 LLM token recovery 的设置与成功率。再看 framework survey，那部分最有工程含量，因为它告诉你哪些常见配置真的会踩坑。\n\n阅读时要避免把它误读成“所有动态量化都有问题”。作者区分得很明确：问题集中在 per-tensor across-batch 的动态量化；静态量化和 per-token dynamic quantization 不在攻击面内。若你关心部署安全，最后再看 mitigation 讨论，判断替换默认量化粒度的代价有多大。\n\n预计阅读时间 20–30 分钟。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper identifies a serving-time privacy vulnerability that is easy to miss: when multiple users are batched together and activations are dynamically quantized using parameters computed across the batch, those shared quantization parameters create a cross-user side channel. An attacker only needs batch co-location and observation of its own output perturbations to infer information about the victim’s input.","importance":"This is not a pretraining-method paper, but it matters for large-model infrastructure and quantized deployment. The key point is that dynamic quantization is often treated as a pure efficiency knob, affecting speed, memory, and perhaps a little accuracy. The paper shows that quantization granularity also changes the privacy boundary in multi-tenant serving. It also delivers a concrete engineering conclusion: per-token dynamic quantization is not only better aligned with LLM activation heterogeneity and often better for GPU execution, it also removes this leakage channel entirely. In contrast, per-tensor online quantization across the batch introduces a structural risk.","technical_approach":"$1d","experiments":"$1e","reading_guide":"$1f","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"预训练本身不用细读；做 serving 或评测量化方案时应看攻击设定，别把动态量化当无状态优化。","en":"Not a pretraining paper, but worth reading if you own serving: dynamic quantization should not be treated as a stateless optimization."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Hanna Foerster","Ilia Shumailov","Cheng Zhang","Yiren Zhao","Jamie Hayes","Robert Mullins"],"notable_authors":["Ilia Shumailov","Jamie Hayes"],"tags":["dynamic-quantization","serving-security","privacy-leakage","batched-inference","side-channel"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26505","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.26505","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2604.26334","title":"Efficient, VRAM-Constrained xLM Inference on Clients","abstract":"To usher in the next round of client AI innovation, there is an urgent need to enable efficient, lossless inference of high-accuracy large language models (LLMs) and vision language models (VLMs), jointly referred to as xLMs, on client systems.
